“우리 말 놓을까요?” (Shall we drop the honorifics?)

In Korea, ‘age’ is not just a number! When you meet someone for the first time, it becomes a very important standard for establishing a social relationship and determining the ‘tone of speech’ towards each other. Shall we enter the complex and subtle world of honorifics and casual language?

**1. 존댓말 vs 반말 (Polite vs Casual)**

- **존댓말 (Honorifics):** Used with people older than you, people you meet for the first time, and in formal settings. As we’ve learned so far, the end of the sentence usually ends in ‘~요’ or ‘~습니다’. (e.g., 밥 먹었어요?)
- **반말 (Casual Language):** This is casual language used comfortably with very close friends or people younger than you. You usually just drop the ‘요’. (e.g., 밥 먹었어?)

**2. 말을 놓다 (Dropping the Honorifics)**

No matter how close you get, if you suddenly use casual language, the other person might be startled or think you’re rude. At times like this, a polite ‘dance’ of asking carefully is required!

- **말 편하게 하셔도 돼요.** (You can speak comfortably/casually to me.) - Used when giving permission to an older person to use casual language with you.
- **우리 말 놓을까요?** (Shall we drop the honorifics?) - The perfect sentence to carefully suggest conversing in casual language when you are the same age or have gotten quite close.

**3. 술자리 예절: 고개 돌리기 (The Polite Head Turn)**

In Korea, there’s a cute etiquette you must follow when drinking with a senior (선배) who is older or higher in rank. When you clink glasses (짠!) and drink, you don’t look directly at the older person; instead, you slightly turn your head to the side and drink while covering your glass with your hand. This is a sign of respect and politeness!

**4. Practice**

✔**Practice 1.** Answer appropriately for the situation.

1. (You’ve met a Korean friend of the same age several times and gotten very close. How would you ask to use casual language instead of honorifics?)
“우리 말 _______________?”
2. (One of the most important questions asked to someone you just met is “나이가 어떻게 되세요?” (How old are you?). After hearing the answer, you’ll know whether to use ( 반말 / 존댓말 ).)
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 놓을까요 / 2. 존댓말 (Always use honorifics at first!)

---


---


---



![[sulsul_pattern_68.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 동사 어간 Verb Stem ]** +**는 김에**
- *“While I am at it (doing something), I will also…”*

### 2) Core Meaning

Used when you are already moving your body to do something and express a ‘killing two birds with one stone’ mindset: “Since I’m doing this anyway, let’s just do that together!”. It’s a perfect pattern for taking full advantage of a situation or opportunity.

### 3) Grammar

- After the present tense of an action verb, use **‘-는 김에’**, and after the past tense, use ‘-(으)ㄴ 김에’.
- Usually, a light request to someone else or your own additional resolution follows in the next sentence.

### 4) How it’s used

- When paths overlap: “편의점 가는 김에 우유도 샀어요.”
- When asking naturally: “나가는 김에 쓰레기 좀 버려줘요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**가는 김에** (While going),**온 김에** (While I’m here),**하는 김에** (While doing it)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **가는 김에** (While going…)
- **온 김에** (While I’m here…)
- **하는 김에** (While doing it…)
- **청소하는 김에** (While cleaning…)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **밖에 나가는 김에 쓰레기 좀 버려 주세요.** (While you are going out, please throw away the trash.)
- **편의점 가는 김에 제 커피도 샀어요.** (While I was going to the convenience store, I bought my coffee too.)
- **한국에 온 김에 맛있는 걸 많이 먹고 갈 거예요.** (While I am in Korea, I’m going to eat a lot of delicious food before I go.)
- **기왕 청소하는 김에 화장실도 같이 하자.** (Since we are cleaning anyway, let’s do the bathroom too.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip (The Magic of Requests)**
> 
> 
> When asking someone to run an errand, if you lay the groundwork by saying “나가는 김에 이것 좀 부탁해~” (While you’re going out, please do this for me~), the other person will feel less bothered and can help you without burden. It’s the Korean soft request technique!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Connect sentences utilizing opportunities.

1. (I’m going to the mart. Please buy some snacks too) 마트 _______________ 과자도 사 오세요.
2. (I traveled to Busan. Let’s see the ocean before we leave) 부산에 _______________ 바다도 보고 갑시다.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 가는 김에 / 2. 온 김에

### 9) My Sentence

What else do you want to do while you have this Korean book open?

- 한국어 책을 _______________ _______________.

## 

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_69.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 동사 어간 Verb Stem ]** +**는 대신에** /**[ 명사 Noun ]** +**대신에**
- *“Instead of (doing/Noun) / In return for (doing)”*

### 2) Core Meaning

Used when you don’t do option A as originally planned and choose a new option B. It is also a very fun and sharp pattern used to offer a reward or strike a ‘Deal’ with a friend, like “I’ll do this for you, so you do that for me”.

### 3) Grammar

- Right after a Noun, attach ‘대신(에)’. (e.g., 밥 대신에)
- After an Action Verb, attach as present tense ‘-는 대신(에)’. (e.g., 도와주는 대신에)
- It’s totally fine to drop the ‘에’ at the very end and just coolly say ‘대신’.

### 4) How it’s used

- When changing a choice: “커피 대신 차를 마실게요.”
- When proposing a deal: “제가 요리하는 대신 설거지해 주세요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**커피 대신에** (Instead of coffee),**가는 대신에** (Instead of going),**밥 대신에** (Instead of rice)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **밥 대신에** (Instead of rice)
- **커피 대신에** (Instead of coffee)
- **가는 대신에** (Instead of going)
- **보는 대신에** (Instead of watching)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **오늘은 커피 대신에 차를 마실게요.** (I will drink tea instead of coffee today.)
- **주말에 영화를 보는 대신에 산책할까요?** (Shall we take a walk instead of watching a movie this weekend?)
- **제가 요리를 하는 대신에 지훈 씨가 설거지를 해 주세요.** (In return for me cooking, Jihun, please do the dishes.)
- **제가 밥을 사는 대신에 네가 커피를 사!** (In return for me buying the meal, you buy the coffee!)

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Suggest alternatives.

1. (Let’s take the bus instead of a taxi) 택시 _______________ 버스를 타고 가요.
2. (I’ll help you with your homework, so you buy me a meal) 내가 숙제를 도와주_______________ 밥 사요.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 대신(에) / 2. 는 대신(에)

### 9) My Sentence

Is there something B you want to do instead of A?

- 저는 _______________ 대신에 _______________.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_70.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 형용사/동사 어간 Adjective/Verb Stem ]** +**아/어도**
- *“Even if / Even though”*

### 2) Core Meaning

It carries the firm meaning that even if the preceding situation is the worst or acts as an obstacle, the following result will not change or will be okay. It’s mainly used to give off a super cool vibe like “I don’t care, I’m going to do it anyway!”.

### 3) Grammar

If the vowel of the verb or adjective stem is ‘ㅏ, ㅗ’, it becomes **‘-아도’**; for the rest, it’s**‘-어도’**; and ‘하다’ becomes ‘해도’.
It is exactly identical in shape to the front part of the pattern we learned when asking for permission (‘-아/어도 돼요?’).

### 4) How it’s used

- When showing willpower: “비가 와도 축구 할 거예요.”
- When you really like an item: “비싸도 꼭 살 거예요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**비가 와도** (Even if it rains),**비싸도** (Even if it’s expensive),**피곤해도** (Even if tired),**어려워도** (Even if difficult)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **와도** (Even if it comes/rains)
- **비싸도** (Even if it’s expensive)
- **피곤해도** (Even if I’m tired)
- **어려워도** (Even if it’s difficult)
- **아파도** (Even if it hurts)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **내일 비가 와도 축구를 할 거예요.** (Even if it rains tomorrow, I will play soccer.)
- **이 가방은 너무 비싸도 꼭 사고 싶어요.** (Even though this bag is expensive, I really want to buy it.)
- **피곤해도 오늘 숙제는 꼭 다 해야 해요.** (Even if you are tired, you must finish your homework today.)
- **한국어가 어려워도 포기하지 않을 거예요.** (Even if Korean is difficult, I won’t give up.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> This comes up ALL the time in Korean drama OSTs or song lyrics. When you hear sorrowful lyrics like “네가 미워도 나는 널 사랑해~” (Even if I hate you, I love you~), you will remember this grammar!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Coolly express your willpower.

1. (I am full, but I will eat dessert) 배가 _______________ 디저트는 먹을 거예요.
2. (The traffic is bad, but I will take the bus) 길이 _______________ 버스를 타고 갈 거예요.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 불러도 / 2. 막혀도

### 9) My Sentence

Is there something you definitely want to accomplish no matter what difficulties you face?

- 저는 _______________아/어도 _______________.

---

 
![[sulsul_pattern_71.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **아무리** +**[ 형용사/동사 어간 Adjective/Verb Stem ]** +**아/어도**
- *“No matter how much I do / No matter how [Adjective] it is”*

### 2) Core Meaning

This is an advanced version of Pattern 70 we just learned! By slapping ‘아무리’ at the front, it immensely emphasizes that the degree of the action is very severe. It expresses strong stubbornness or limits, meaning “Even if the world splits in two, even if I try ten times, it won’t work!”.

### 3) Grammar

**‘아무리’** must ALWAYS be paired with ‘-아/어도’ as a complete set to form a perfect sentence.
There are many idiomatic expressions used as entire chunks like ‘아무리 생각해도’ (No matter how much I think about it) and ‘아무리 찾아도’ (No matter how much I search).

### 4) How it’s used

- When feeling a limit: “아무리 찾아도 지갑이 없어요.”
- When stating a firm routine: “아무리 바빠도 밥은 먹어요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**아무리 바빠도** (No matter how busy),**아무리 찾아도** (No matter how much I search),**아무리 힘들어도** (No matter how hard)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **아무리 바빠도** (No matter how busy)
- **아무리 찾아도** (No matter how much I search)
- **아무리 힘들어도** (No matter how hard)
- **아무리 먹어도** (No matter how much I eat)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **저는 아무리 바빠도 아침은 꼭 챙겨 먹어요.** (No matter how busy I am, I always make sure to eat breakfast.)
- **방을 아무리 찾아도 제 지갑이 없어요.** (No matter how much I search the room, my wallet isn’t there.)
- **지금 아무리 힘들어도 절대 포기하지 마세요.** (No matter how hard it is right now, never give up.)
- **아무리 생각해도 무슨 뜻인지 잘 모르겠어요.** (No matter how much I think about it, I really don’t know what it means.)

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Emphasize an extreme situation.

1. (I ate a lot, but I’m still hungry) _______________ 먹어도 배가 고파요.
2. (Even if they give me a lot of money, I won’t do that job) 아무리 돈이 _______________ 그 일은 안 해요.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 아무리 / 2. 많아도

### 9) My Sentence

What is one thing you absolutely never skip in a day, no matter how tired or busy you are?

- 저는 아무리 _______________아/어도 _______________.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_72.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 동사 어간 Verb Stem ]** +**고 보니까 / 다 보니까**
- *“Now that I’ve done it / After doing it, I realized…”*

### 2) Core Meaning

Used when you do an action and then suddenly realize a new fact saying “Ah, now I see!”, or when an unexpected result appears. It beautifully expresses that thrilling (?) or embarrassing moment when you directly experience and realize something you didn’t know before doing it.

### 3) Grammar

- It ONLY attaches to **Action Verbs**.
- **‘-고 보니까’:** Describes a realization made instantly after finishing an action ONCE.
- **‘-다 보니까’:** Describes a natural result discovered while continuously ‘repeating’ an action.
- In daily conversation, people drop the ‘까’ of ‘보니까’ and say ‘-고 보니 / -다 보니’ much more often!

### 4) How it’s used

- When realizing a mistake too late: “먹고 보니까 유통기한이 지났어요.”
- When growing fond of something through repetition: “계속 듣다 보니까 이 노래가 좋아요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**사고 보니까** (After buying I realized),**만나고 보니까** (After meeting I realized),**듣다 보니까** (As I kept listening)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **사고 보니까** (After buying it I realized…)
- **만나고 보니까** (After meeting them I realized…)
- **듣다 보니까** (As I kept listening…)
- **살다 보니까** (As I kept living there…)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **우유를 마시고 보니까 유통기한이 지났어요.** (After drinking the milk, I realized it was past the expiration date.)
- **처음엔 몰랐는데 대화하고 보니까 아주 좋은 사람이었어요.** (I didn’t know at first, but after talking to him, I realized he was a very good person.)
- **계속 듣다 보니까 이 노래가 점점 좋아졌어요.** (After continuously listening to it, I gradually came to like this song.)
- **한국에서 살다 보니까 매운 음식을 잘 먹게 됐어요.** (As I kept living in Korea, I became able to eat spicy food well.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> When you make an impulse purchase and regret it upon returning home, put your hand on your forehead and talk to yourself saying, “아, 사고 보니까 별로네…” (Ah, sa-go bo-ni-kka byeol-lo-ne / Ah, after buying it, I realize it’s not that great…). You are a perfect native speaker.
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** State your moment of realization.

1. (I didn’t study Korean just once, but kept doing it. Then I found it fun) 한국어를 계속 공부하_______________ 재미있어요.
2. (I bought clothes and tried them on at home. Then I found them small) 옷을 사_______________ 사이즈가 작아요.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 다 보니까 (or 다 보니) / 2. 고 보니까 (or 고 보니)

### 9) My Sentence

Is there something you didn’t like at first, but grew to like as you kept doing it?

- 처음에는 싫었는데, 계속 _______________다 보니까 좋아졌어요.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_73.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 동사 어간 Verb Stem ]** +**다 보면**
- *“If you keep doing (something)…”*

### 2) Core Meaning

It acts like a magic spell used to encourage and assure someone that if they ‘keep repeating’ an action without giving up, a good result or positive change will definitely happen later.

### 3) Grammar

Attach **‘-다 보면’** right after an action verb stem.
In the following sentence, words that indicate a hopeful and positive change in the future, such as ‘-게 될 거예요 (will become)’ or ‘-을 수 있을 거예요 (will be able to)’, fit perfectly.

### 4) How it’s used

- When encouraging and comforting someone: “매일 연습하다 보면 잘할 거예요.”
- When sharing life’s wisdom: “살다 보면 좋은 날이 와요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**연습하다 보면** (If you keep practicing),**살다 보면** (As you go through life),**걷다 보면** (If you keep walking)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **연습하다 보면** (If you keep practicing…)
- **살다 보면** (As you keep living…)
- **하다 보면** (If you keep doing it…)
- **걷다 보면** (If you keep walking…)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **매일 연습하다 보면 분명히 잘하게 될 거예요.** (If you keep practicing every day, you will definitely become good at it.)
- **한국 드라마를 자주 하다 보면 한국어가 들릴 거예요.** (If you keep watching Korean dramas, you will be able to hear Korean.)
- **쭉 걷다 보면 오른쪽에 우체국이 보일 거예요.** (If you keep walking straight, you will see a post office on the right.)
- **살다 보면 이렇게 힘든 날도 있는 거죠.** (As you go through life, there are hard days like this too.)

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Predict a hopeful result.

1. (Memorize Korean words every day. Then you will know them) 단어를 매일 _______________ 외워질 거예요.
2. (Keep looking for the way. Then you’ll see it) 계속 _______________ 찾을 수 있을 거예요.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 외우다 보면 / 2. 찾다 보면

### 9) My Sentence

Say some warm words of encouragement to a friend struggling with Korean.

- 조금 어렵지만, 계속 _______________다 보면 잘할 수 있을 거예요!

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_74.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 형용사/동사 어간 Adjective/Verb Stem ]** +**기 마련이에요**
- *“It is bound to / It is natural that…”*

### 2) Core Meaning

Used when stating an unavoidable and extremely natural providence, saying “That’s how the world works, it’s natural~”. It’s a pattern to use when you want to speak very maturely and coolly, as if stating a truth or law of life.

### 3) Grammar

Attach **‘-기 마련이다 / -기 마련이에요’** directly to the verb or adjective stem regardless of the Batchim.
It becomes much clearer in meaning when used with a conditional clause like ‘-(으)면’.

### 4) How it’s used

- When comforting a friend who made a mistake: “처음엔 다 실수하기 마련이에요.”
- When stating an inevitable result: “노력하면 성공하기 마련이죠.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**실수하기 마련이에요** (Bound to make mistakes),**춥기 마련이에요** (Bound to be cold),**잊어버리기 마련이에요** (Bound to forget)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **실수하기 마련이에요.** (It is bound to make mistakes.)
- **춥기 마련이에요.** (It is bound to be cold.)
- **성공하기 마련이에요.** (It is bound to succeed.)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **사람은 누구나 처음에는 실수하기 마련이에요. 너무 걱정 마세요.** (It is natural for anyone to make mistakes at first. Don’t worry too much.)
- **겨울이 오면 날씨가 춥기 마련이에요.** (It is bound to be cold when winter comes.)
- **끝까지 포기하지 않고 노력하면 성공하기 마련이에요.** (If you don’t give up and make an effort until the end, you are bound to succeed.)
- **시간이 지나면 슬픈 기억도 다 잊어버리기 마련이죠.** (As time passes, sad memories are naturally bound to be forgotten.)

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** State the natural order of the world.

1. (If you keep lying, you will eventually be caught) 거짓말은 언젠가 _______________.
2. (As you get older, your health deteriorates) 나이가 들면 건강이 _______________.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 들키기 마련이에요 / 2. 나빠지기 마련이에요

### 9) My Sentence

What do you think is a natural truth or law of life?

- 사람은 누구나 _______________기 마련이에요.

## 💎 Special Unit 7. 배달의 민족 (Delivery Culture)

"문 앞에 두고 벨 눌러주세요" (Please leave it at the door and ring the bell)

Korea is the undisputed global champion of food delivery! From midnight fried chicken at the Han River park to a single cup of iced coffee delivered right to your door, everything is possible. Let's master the essential terms and culture to fully enjoy Korea's delivery ecosystem.

**1. 배달 필수 단어 (Essential Delivery Vocabulary)**

*   **배달비 (Delivery Fee):** The extra charge for delivery. Koreans always compare this fee before ordering!
*   **최소 주문 금액 (Minimum Order Amount):** The minimum amount of money you must spend to place an order.
*   **리뷰 이벤트 (Review Event):** A unique Korean culture! If you promise to leave a 5-star review with a photo on the app, the restaurant gives you free side dishes or drinks (called '서비스').

**2. 한강 치맥 (Han River "Chimaek" Delivery)**

You don't need a home address to get delivery in Korea. If you are chilling at a park along the Han River, you can order chicken and beer ("치맥 - Chimaek"). 

When ordering, you just set your location to the nearest**배달존 (Delivery Zone)** (e.g., Yeouido Park Delivery Zone 2). The driver will call you when they arrive, and you just walk over to pick up your food!

**3. 센스 있는 요청 사항 (Polite Delivery Requests)**

Koreans value speed and privacy. When ordering through an app like '배달의민족 (Baemin)', the most common requests written in the delivery note are:
*   "문 앞에 두고 벨 눌러주세요." (Please leave it in front of the door and ring the bell.)
*   "조심히 안전하게 오세요." (Please drive safely.)

**Practice**

✔**Practice 1.** Fill in the blanks with the correct delivery terms.

1. (I want free cheese balls. I should participate in this) _______________에 참여할게요!
2. (Ordering chicken and beer at the Han River park) 우리 한강에서 _______________ 배달시켜 먹을까요?
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 리뷰 이벤트 / 2. 치맥

---


---


---



![[sulsul_pattern_75.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 동사 어간 Verb Stem ]** +**기만 하면**
- *“Whenever (someone does this) / As long as (someone does this)”*

### 2) Core Meaning

Used when magically the ‘same’ result always appears if just one specific action is taken. It’s fun to use when talking about a terrible jinx or an exceptionless repetitive pattern, like “If I just drink coffee, I absolutely can’t sleep!”.

### 3) Grammar

Attach the noun form **‘-기’** to an action verb, and then stick**‘만 하면’** right after it.
The following sentence must describe a habit that repeats constantly or a 100% expected result, not something that happens once in a while.

### 4) How it’s used

- When stating your physical trait: “커피 마시기만 하면 잠을 못 자요.”
- When stating someone’s jinx: “그 친구는 술을 마시기만 하면 울어요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**마시기만 하면** (Whenever drinking),**듣기만 하면** (Whenever listening),**타기만 하면** (Whenever riding)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **마시기만 하면** (Whenever I drink…)
- **듣기만 하면** (Whenever I listen…)
- **타기만 하면** (Whenever I ride…)
- **보기만 하면** (Whenever I see…)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **저는 늦게 커피를 마시기만 하면 밤에 잠을 못 자요.** (Whenever I drink coffee late, I can’t sleep at night.)
- **내 친구는 술을 마시기만 하면 계속 울어요.** (Whenever my friend drinks alcohol, he cries continuously.)
- **이 신나는 노래를 듣기만 하면 스트레스가 풀리고 기분이 좋아져요.** (Whenever I listen to this exciting song, my stress is relieved and I feel good.)
- **저는 차를 타기만 하면 멀미를 해서 너무 힘들어요.** (Whenever I ride in a car, I get motion sickness so it’s very hard.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> It’s much stronger in meaning than the general conditional ‘-(으)면’. Pull this pattern out when you want to strongly convey the feeling of ‘It happens 100% of the time, unconditionally, without exception!’.
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Set a condition with no exceptions.

1. (I get sleepy when I eat. It’s 100% like that) 저는 밥을 _______________ 졸려요.
2. (Those two fight whenever they see each other’s faces) 그 두 사람은 얼굴을 _______________ 싸워요.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 먹기만 하면 / 2. 보기만 하면

### 9) My Sentence

Do you have a jinx or pattern that always happens unconditionally when you do a certain action?

- 저는 _______________기만 하면 _______________.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_76.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 동사 어간 Verb Stem ]** +**아/어 가다 / 아/어 오다**
- *“Has been doing / Will keep doing (progress over time)”*

### 2) Core Meaning

This is a very cool and poetic expression that shows the ‘flow of time’ like a video clip, meaning you have been continuously doing something from the past until now (**아/어 오다**) or you will keep doing something from now into the future (**아/어 가다**).

### 3) Grammar

- **It ONLY attaches to Action Verbs (Verb)!** You cannot attach it directly to adjectives. (e.g., 작아 가다 - X)
- If you want to use an adjective, you must first turn it into a state-change verb using ‘-아/어지다’ learned in Pattern 58. (e.g., 작다(Adj) → 작아지다(Verb) →**작아져 가다(O)**)
- **‘-아/어 오다’** is mostly used for events from the past to the present, and**‘-아/어 가다’** is used for the present to the future, or for things that are nearing completion.

### 4) How it’s used

- When appealing to past efforts: “한국어를 꾸준히 공부해 왔어요.”
- When work is nearing completion: “준비가 거의 다 끝나 가요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**공부해 왔어요** (Have been studying),**끝나 가요** (Is getting finished),**변해 왔어요** (Has been changing)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **공부해 왔어요.** (Have been studying.)
- **끝나 가요.** (Getting finished.)
- **살아갈 거예요.** (Will keep living.)
- **변해 왔어요.** (Has been changing.)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **저는 한국어를 1년 동안 열심히 공부해 왔어요.** (I have been studying Korean diligently for a year.)
- **파티 준비가 거의 다 끝나 가요. 조금만 기다리세요.** (The party preparation is almost getting finished. Please wait a little.)
- **앞으로도 우리는 변함없이 서로 사랑하며 살아갈 거예요.** (We will keep living loving each other without changing from now on.)
- **세상은 생각보다 아주 빠르게 변해 왔어요.** (The world has been changing much faster than we thought.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> When someone is rushing you from outside the room asking “다 했어?” (Are you done?), try shouting “거의 다 해 가요!” (da hae ga-yo). It becomes a very native-like response meaning, “Wait a little bit, it’s getting done~”.
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Create a flow of time.

1. (I have continuously eaten at that restaurant from the past until now) 옛날부터 지금까지 그 식당에서 밥을 _______________.
2. (Room cleaning is 90% done) 방 청소가 거의 다 _______________.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 먹어 왔어요 / 2. 끝나 가요

### 9) My Sentence

What is something you have been steadily doing without giving up for a long time until now?

- 저는 오랫동안 _______________아/어 왔어요.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_77.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 동사 어간 Verb Stem ]** +**는 동안** /**[ 명사 Noun ]** +**동안**
- *“While (doing)” / “During [Noun]”*

### 2) Core Meaning

Used when another event happens at the same time within the ‘length’ of time a certain action is continuing. It’s highly useful when two situations overlap in time, like “What happened while I was sleeping?”.

### 3) Grammar

- After a Noun, just stick **‘동안’** right on it. (e.g., 방학 동안, 3일 동안)
- After an Action Verb, attach the present modifier**‘-는’** to make**‘-는 동안’**.
- The most convenient part: The subjects (people) of the first sentence and the second sentence can be different! (While I cook, you rest!)

### 4) How it’s used

- When explaining a period: “방학 동안 여행 다녀왔어요.”
- When dividing tasks: “제가 요리하는 동안 텔레비전 보세요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**방학 동안** (During vacation),**자는 동안** (While sleeping),**기다리는 동안** (While waiting),**요리하는 동안** (While cooking)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **방학 동안** (During vacation)
- **3일 동안** (For 3 days)
- **자는 동안** (While sleeping)
- **기다리는 동안** (While waiting)
- **요리하는 동안** (While cooking)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **여름 방학 동안 유럽으로 긴 여행을 다녀왔어요.** (I went on a long trip to Europe during the summer vacation.)
- **제가 부엌에서 요리하는 동안 소파에서 텔레비전을 보세요.** (While I am cooking in the kitchen, please watch TV on the sofa.)
- **친구가 화장실 앞에서 기다리는 동안 저는 커피를 마셨어요.** (While my friend was waiting in front of the restroom, I drank coffee.)
- **비행기를 타고 한국으로 가는 동안 푹 잤어요.** (While I was going to Korea on the airplane, I slept soundly.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> **‘(V)을/ㄹ 때 (when)’** that we learned before refers to a pinpoint, momentary ‘dot’ of time like taking a snapshot, but **‘동안 (while/during)’** means a ‘line’ of time with length, like recording a video. Make sure to remember this difference in nuance!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** State events that happened simultaneously.

1. (Staying in Korea for 1 year) 한국에 1년 _______________ 많은 곳을 구경했어요.
2. (I will clean, you do the dishes!) 내가 청소_______________ 너는 설거지해.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. (머무르는) 동안 / 2. 하는 동안

### 9) My Sentence

Suggest to your roommate or family member: “I’ll do this, so you do that during that time!”.

- 제가 _______________는 동안 _______________으세요.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_78.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 동사 어간 Verb Stem ]** +**을/ㄹ 만하다**
- *“It is worth (doing) / It’s bearable”*

### 2) Core Meaning

Used when recommending a restaurant or a movie, confidently praising it by saying, “It’s fully worth investing my time and money!”. Conversely, it’s also used to say, “It’s a bit hard, but it’s still bearable.” It’s a versatile expression incredibly useful when leaving evaluations or reviews!

### 3) Grammar

If the action verb stem has a Batchim, attach **‘-을 만하다’**; if not, attach**‘-ㄹ 만하다’**. (Exactly identical to the future tense rules!)
It is frequently paired with specific verbs used in reviews, like ‘볼 만하다’, ‘먹을 만하다’, ‘갈 만하다’.

### 4) How it’s used

- When strongly recommending: “이 영화 진짜 볼 만해요.”
- When evaluating something as ‘not bad’: “이 떡볶이 먹을 만하네요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**볼 만해요** (Worth watching),**먹을 만해요** (Worth eating),**갈 만해요** (Worth going/visiting),**살 만해요** (Worth buying/bearable to live)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **볼 만해요.** (Worth watching.)
- **먹을 만해요.** (Worth eating.)
- **갈 만해요.** (Worth going.)
- **살 만해요.** (Worth buying / Bearable to live.)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **이 영화 액션이 진짜 볼 만해요. 꼭 보세요!** (The action in this movie is really worth watching. Make sure to watch it!)
- **제주도는 살면서 꼭 한 번 갈 만한 곳이에요.** (Jeju Island is a place worth visiting at least once in your life.)
- **매운 떡볶이인데 물을 마시면 먹을 만해요.** (It’s spicy tteokbokki, but if you drink water, it’s bearable to eat.)
- **한국의 여름은 너무 덥지만 에어컨이 있어서 살 만해요.** (Summer in Korea is too hot, but because there are air conditioners, it’s bearable to live.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip (Native Skill)**
> 
> 
> Sometimes, if “먹을 만해요” is written in a restaurant review, rather than being massive praise, it might carry a mid-level nuance of “Hmm, it’s not bad, it meets the standard.” When you really, truly want to recommend something, put emphasis adverbs in front like “진짜 볼 만해요!” (It’s really worth watching!).
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Make a recommendation containing your opinion.

1. (This drama is fun, so it’s worth investing 1 hour to watch) 이 드라마는 _______________.
2. (These shoes have a pretty design, so they’re worth paying 100,000 won to buy) 이 신발은 _______________.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 볼 만해요 / 2. 살 만해요

### 9) My Sentence

Recommend a place from your hometown or travel destinations that is truly worth visiting at least once.

- _______________은/는 정말 한 번쯤 _______________을/ㄹ 만한 곳이에요.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_79.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 동사 어간 Verb Stem ]** +**는 한**
- *“As long as (condition continues)”*

### 2) Core Meaning

Used to swear or declare that as long as the preceding condition does not collapse and is maintained, the following result will be firmly protected without any change. It feels like drawing an invisible thick line. It gives a slightly resolute yet confident feeling!

### 3) Grammar

- Attach the present modifier **‘-는’** after an action verb, add a space, and write**‘한’**.
- It feels slightly more serious and sets a restricted boundary compared to ‘-기만 하면 (Pattern 75)’ we learned earlier.
- Usually, the following sentence contains contents like ‘It will never change’ or ‘It will continue’.

### 4) How it’s used

- When confessing romantically: “내가 살아있는 한 널 지킬게.”
- When stating firm willpower: “포기하지 않는 한 실패는 없어요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**살아있는 한** (As long as I’m alive),**포기하지 않는 한** (As long as you don’t give up),**있는 한** (As long as it’s there)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **살아있는 한** (As long as I’m alive…)
- **포기하지 않는 한** (As long as you don’t give up…)
- **있는 한** (As long as it’s there…)
- **하는 한** (As long as you do it…)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **내가 살아있는 한 너를 끝까지 지켜줄게.** (As long as I am alive, I will protect you to the end.)
- **스스로 포기하지 않는 한 우리에게 완벽한 실패는 없어요.** (As long as you don’t give up yourself, there is no complete failure for us.)
- **한국에 있는 한 계속 한국어를 열심히 공부할 거예요.** (As long as I am in Korea, I will keep studying Korean diligently.)
- **이 회사를 다니는 한 주말에는 쉴 수 없어요.** (As long as I work for this company, I can’t rest on weekends.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> This is a line used very often by male leads in Korean dramas when confessing to the female lead in the rain. Try saying “내가 네 곁에 있는 한…” (As long as I am by your side…) romantically with feeling!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Draw a firm limit line.

1. (I make money. I will keep buying you delicious things) 내가 돈을 _______________ 계속 사줄게.
2. (You help me. No problem) 네가 나를 _______________ 문제없어.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 버는 한 / 2. 돕는 한

### 9) My Sentence

Express your firm willpower coolly by drawing a ‘limit line’.

- 제가 _______________는 한, 절대 _______________.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_80.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 형용사/동사 어간 Adjective/Verb Stem ]** +** (으)ㄴ/는 척하다**
- *“Pretend to (be / do)”*

### 2) Core Meaning

This is a very fun expression used when you do some ‘Acting’ outwardly while it’s not actually the truth. Whether pretending to know something you don’t, or pretending not to know something you know very well, your delicate acting skills are 100% described with this single pattern.

### 3) Grammar

You just attach **‘척하다’** to the rule for modifying nouns (Pattern 33)!

- **Verbs:** Attach**‘-는 척하다’** after the present tense, and ‘-(으)ㄴ 척하다’ after the past tense. (e.g., 자는 척하다, 잔 척하다)
- **Adjectives:** Attach**‘-(으)ㄴ 척하다’** depending on the Batchim. (e.g., 예쁜 척하다, 좋은 척하다)

### 4) How it’s used

- When pointing out someone’s acting: “자는 척하지 마세요!”
- When letting something slide out of consideration: “알면서 모르는 척했어요.”
- When showing fake emotions: “괜찮은 척했어요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**자는 척하다** (Pretend to sleep),**모르는 척하다** (Pretend not to know),**예쁜 척하다** (Pretend to be pretty),**괜찮은 척하다** (Pretend to be okay)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **자는 척했어요.** (Pretended to sleep.)
- **모르는 척했어요.** (Pretended not to know.)
- **예쁜 척해요.** (Pretends to be pretty.)
- **괜찮은 척해요.** (Pretends to be okay.)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **지금 다 들리니까 자는 척하지 말고 일어나세요!** (I can hear everything, so stop pretending to sleep and get up!)
- **길에서 그 사람을 봤지만 부끄러워서 모르는 척했어요.** (I saw that person on the street, but I was shy so I pretended not to know them.)
- **사실 마음이 아팠지만 친구 앞에서는 괜찮은 척했어요.** (Actually my heart hurt, but in front of my friend I pretended to be okay.)
- **자기가 다 아는 척하면서 잘난 척 좀 그만해요!** (Stop showing off pretending you know everything!)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> When joking around among friends, “예쁜 척하지 마!” (Stop pretending to be pretty!) and “잘난 척 좀 그만해!” (Stop showing off!) are very frequently used practical ping-pong conversational expressions.
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Create a sentence about perfect acting.

1. (I was very scared but acted like nothing was wrong) 무서웠지만 안 무서_______________. (무섭다)
2. (They have no money but act like they have a lot of money) 돈이 _______________. (많다)
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 운 척했어요 / 2. 많은 척해요

### 9) My Sentence

Have you ever acted outwardly differently from your true feelings?

- 저는 _______________지만 _______________ 척했어요.

---


![[survival_mission_6.png|700]]


![[chapter_07.png|700]]

![[sulsul_pattern_81.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 형용사/동사 어간 Adjective/Verb Stem ]** +**을/ㄹ수록**
- *“The more you [Action/State], the more…”*

### 2) Core Meaning

This pattern means “As time passes, or the more you repeat an action, the severity or degree of it gradually increases.” It plays exactly the same role as the English structure “The more ~, the more ~”!

### 3) Grammar

- **If there is a Batchim:** -을수록 (e.g., 먹다 →**먹을수록**, 좋다 →**좋을수록**)
- **If no Batchim or ‘ㄹ’ Batchim:** -ㄹ수록 (e.g., 가다 →**갈수록**, 만들다 →**만들수록**)
- Usually, repeating the same verb twice in the form of**“-(으)면 -(으)ㄹ수록”** strongly emphasizes the meaning! (e.g., 많으면 많을수록 좋아요 / The more there is, the better).

### 4) How it’s used

- When talking about charm: “한국어는 배울수록 재미있어요.”
- When stating desired conditions: “집은 넓으면 넓을수록 좋아요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**갈수록** (As time goes by),**볼수록** (The more I see),**많을수록** (The more there are)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **갈수록** (As time goes by / More and more)
- **볼수록** (The more I see it)
- **먹을수록** (The more I eat it)
- **많을수록** (The more there are)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **한국어는 배울수록 재미있어요.** (The more I learn Korean, the more fun it is.)
- **갈수록 날씨가 추워져요.** (As time goes on, the weather gets colder.)
- **이 책은 읽을수록 어려워요.** (The more I read this book, the harder it is.)
- **돈은 많으면 많을수록 좋아요.** (The more money there is, the better.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> **“갈수록”** originally comes from “가다 (to go)”, but it has solidified into an adverb and is very frequently used as a single word meaning **“점점 더 (as time goes by / more and more)”**!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Express a growing feeling.

1. (The more I look at that person, the more charming they are) 그 사람을 _______________ 매력 있어요.
2. (Because I keep eating spicy food, it’s getting spicier) 매운 음식을 _______________ 매워져요.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 볼수록 (or 보면 볼수록) / 2. 먹을수록 (or 먹으면 먹을수록)

### 9) My Sentence

Is there something you like more and more as you do it?

- _______________은/는 _______________을/ㄹ수록 더 좋아요.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_82.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 형용사/동사 어간 Adjective/Verb Stem ]** +**을/ㄹ 리가 없어요**
- *“There is no way that…”, “It’s impossible that…”*

### 2) Core Meaning

This is an expression used when strongly doubting and 100% denying a situation by saying, “There is absolutely no way that’s true!”. You use it firmly when you think an event is utterly impossible based on your knowledge or common sense.

### 3) Grammar

- **If there is a Batchim:** -을 리가 없어요 (e.g., 작다 →**작을 리가 없어요**)
- **If no Batchim or ‘ㄹ’ Batchim:** -ㄹ 리가 없어요 (e.g., 비싸다 →**비쌀 리가 없어요**)
- After a Noun, use “-(이)라는 리가 없어요” or “일 리가 없어요”. (e.g., 학생일 리가 없어요 / There’s no way they are a student)

### 4) How it’s used

- When hearing unbelievable news: “그 사람이 거짓말을 할 리가 없어요.”
- When clearing up a misunderstanding: “제가 그런 말을 했을 리가 없잖아요!”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**그럴 리가 없어요** (That can’t be true),**없을 리가 없어요** (It can’t be missing),**모를 리가 없어요** (There’s no way they don’t know)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **그럴 리가 없어요.** (There is no way that’s true.)
- **없을 리가 없어요.** (There is no way it’s not here.)
- **모를 리가 없어요.** (There is no way they don’t know.)
- **비쌀 리가 없어요.** (There is no way it’s expensive.)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **그 사람이 거짓말을 할 리가 없어요.** (There’s no way that person would lie.)
- **이 가격이 맞을 리가 없어요. 너무 싸요!** (There is no way this price is right. It’s too cheap!)
- **벌써 도착했을 리가 없어요.** (There’s no way they arrived already. - Past tense)
- **지민 씨가 이 사실을 모를 리가 없어요.** (There’s no way Jimin doesn’t know about this fact.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> In speaking, people often shorten “리가” to just “리” and say **“그럴 리 없어요~”**!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Strongly deny an unbelievable situation.

1. (A friend who usually sleeps early is not sleeping yet? No way!) 그 친구가 아직 안 _______________. (자다)
2. (My wallet is not in my bag? I definitely packed it) 내 지갑이 _______________. (없다)
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 잘 리가 없어요 / 2. 없을 리가 없어요

### 9) My Sentence

What is something recently that made you think, “Nonsense, there’s absolutely no way!”?

- _______________이/가 _______________을/ㄹ 리가 없어요.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_83.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 동사 어간 Verb Stem ]** +**자마자**
- *“As soon as…”, “Right after…”*

### 2) Core Meaning

It means that as soon as one action finishes, the next action happens immediately without even a second of delay. It is used to dynamically emphasize very fast speed or an immediate reaction.

### 3) Grammar

- It can **ONLY attach to Verbs**. (Not adjectives!)
- Just attach “-자마자” regardless of whether there’s a Batchim or not.
- **Caution:** Even when talking about a past event, NEVER use the past tense marker in front of ‘-자마자’! The tense is always indicated only in the sentence that follows. (e.g., 먹었자마자 (X) →**먹자마자 잠들었어요 (O)**).

### 4) How it’s used

- When stating an urgent schedule: “도착하자마자 전화할게요.”
- When describing a quick result: “눕자마자 잠들었어요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**도착하자마자** (As soon as I arrive),**끝나자마자** (As soon as it finishes),**눈을 뜨자마자** (As soon as I open my eyes)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **도착하자마자** (As soon as I arrive)
- **끝나자마자** (As soon as it finishes)
- **먹자마자** (As soon as I eat)
- **보자마자** (As soon as I see)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **집에 도착하자마자 잠들었어요.** (I fell asleep as soon as I arrived home.)
- **비가 그치자마자 출발할 거예요.** (I will leave as soon as the rain stops.)
- **밥을 먹자마자 운동하면 안 돼요.** (You shouldn’t exercise right after eating.)
- **그 옷을 보자마자 너무 마음에 들었어요.** (As soon as I saw those clothes, I really liked them.)

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Move to the next action without a second’s delay.

1. (I will go eat as soon as class finishes) 수업이 _______________ 밥 먹으러 갈 거예요. (끝나다)
2. (I took a shower right after going home) 집에 _______________ 샤워했어요. (가다)
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 끝나자마자 / 2. 가자마자

### 9) My Sentence

What is the very first thing you did as soon as you opened your eyes in bed this morning?

- 저는 오늘 아침에 눈을 뜨_______________ _______________.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_84.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 동사 어간 Verb Stem ]** +**을/ㄹ 겸 (해서)**
- *“While I’m at it…”, “To do A and also B”*

### 2) Core Meaning

Used when trying to achieve two or more purposes simultaneously while doing a single action. Meaning “to do this and also that,” it’s a perfect pattern that fits the ‘killing two birds with one stone’ situations of busy modern people!

### 3) Grammar

- **If there is a Batchim:** -을 겸 (e.g., 먹다 →**먹을 겸**)
- **If no Batchim or ‘ㄹ’ Batchim:** -ㄹ 겸 (e.g., 쉬다 →**쉴 겸**, 만들다 →**만들 겸**)
- Attach “겸” directly after a Noun. (e.g., 아침 겸 점심 = Brunch)
- You can omit “해서” and just use “-(으)ㄹ 겸” and it’s perfectly fine.

### 4) How it’s used

- When casually giving an excuse for a purpose: “친구도 만날 겸 서울에 왔어요.”
- When stating a light intention: “산책도 할 겸 나왔어요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**만날 겸** (To meet &…),**쉴 겸** (To rest &…),**운동할 겸** (To exercise &…)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **만날 겸** (To meet and…)
- **쉴 겸** (To rest and…)
- **할 겸** (To do and…)
- **운동할 겸** (To exercise and…)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **친구도 만날 겸 서울에 왔어요.** (I came to Seoul to meet my friend - and do other things too.)
- **산책도 할 겸 커피를 사러 나갔어요.** (I went out to take a walk and buy coffee at the same time.)
- **한국어도 연습할 겸 한국 드라마를 봐요.** (I watch Korean dramas to practice Korean - along with just enjoying it.)
- **스트레스도 풀 겸 코인 노래방에 갈래요?** (Shall we go to the coin karaoke to relieve stress while we’re at it?)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> When you want to clearly state two or more purposes, it sounds very natural to use it twice in a row: **“(V)을/ㄹ 겸, (V)을/ㄹ 겸”**!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Combine two purposes into one.

1. (I walked here because I needed to diet and also wanted to get some fresh air) 다이어트도 _______________ 걸어왔어요. (하다)
2. (I work part-time to earn money and also gain experience) 돈도 _______________ 아르바이트를 해요. (벌다)
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 할 겸 / 2. 벌 겸

### 9) My Sentence

Is there another goal you want to achieve simultaneously while reading this Korean book?

- 저는 _______________을/ㄹ 겸 이 책을 읽어요.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_85.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 명사 Noun ]** +**치고(는)**
- *“For a [Noun]…”, “Considering it’s [Noun]…”*

### 2) Core Meaning

There’s usually a general standard people think of regarding a certain group or category, right? This expression is used when something completely exceeds that standard or expectation. If you say “외국인 치고 한국어를 잘해요” (You speak Korean well for a foreigner), it’s a massive compliment meaning you have far surpassed the ‘average skill of foreigners’.

### 3) Grammar

It attaches right after a noun. Always use **“-치고”** or “-치고는” regardless of the Batchim. The “는” is often omitted as it’s just for emphasis.

### 4) How it’s used

- When complimenting: “처음 만든 것 치고는 정말 맛있어요!”
- When stating it’s different from expectations: “겨울 날씨 치고 안 추워요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**외국인 치고는** (For a foreigner),**처음 치고는** (For the first time),**겨울 치고는** (For winter)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **외국인 치고** (For a foreigner)
- **처음 치고** (For the first time)
- **겨울 치고** (For winter)
- **아이 치고** (For a child)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **겨울 날씨 치고 별로 안 추워요.** (For winter weather, it’s not very cold.)
- **처음 만든 것 치고는 정말 맛있네요!** (Considering it’s your first time making it, it’s really delicious!)
- **농구 선수 치고 키가 작은 편이에요.** (For a basketball player, he is on the shorter side.)
- **외국인 치고 한국어 발음이 진짜 완벽해요.** (For a foreigner, your Korean pronunciation is truly perfect.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> If used in the format **“Noun + 치고 + (Negative Word) + 없다”**, it becomes a 100% positive meaning that “ALL nouns are like that”.
> Example: 한국인 치고 김치 안 먹는 사람 없어요! (Meaning: Absolutely every Korean eats Kimchi!)
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Express an exception that goes beyond the standard.

1. (He’s an elementary school student, but he’s 170cm tall) 초등학생 _______________ 키가 아주 커요.
2. (It’s a cheap restaurant, but the food is very delicious) 식당 가격이 싼 것 _______________ 음식이 아주 맛있어요.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 치고 (or 치고는) / 2. 치고 (or 치고는)

### 9) My Sentence

Among the things you’ve tried for the first time, was there something that turned out quite well ‘considering it was your first time’?

- 저는 _______________ 처음 한 것 치고는 아주 잘했어요.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_86.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 동사 어간 Verb Stem ]** +**는 바람에**
- *“Because of [Verb] (unexpectedly)…”, “As a result of [Verb]…”*

### 2) Core Meaning

This is the ultimate pattern for making excuses! It’s used when an unexpected or sudden event becomes the cause, and a ‘bad result’ bursts out at the end. It’s very useful when a perfect plan suddenly goes wrong!

### 3) Grammar

- It can **ONLY attach to Verbs**. Always use “-는 바람에” regardless of the Batchim.
- **Most important point:** Because it’s a sudden event, the following sentence MUST always come with the ‘past tense (-았/었어요)’ as the result, and generally, a**negative result** must follow to sound natural.

### 4) How it’s used

- When you’re late: “늦잠을 자는 바람에 기차를 놓쳤어요.”
- When there’s an accident: “비가 오는 바람에 옷이 젖었어요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**늦잠을 자는 바람에** (Because I overslept),**비가 오는 바람에** (Because it rained suddenly),**고장 나는 바람에** (Because it broke down)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **늦잠을 자는 바람에** (Because I overslept…)
- **비가 오는 바람에** (Because it rained suddenly…)
- **넘어지는 바람에** (Because I fell down…)
- **고장 나는 바람에** (Because it broke down…)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **갑자기 비가 오는 바람에 옷이 다 젖었어요.** (Because it rained unexpectedly, my clothes got all wet.)
- **늦잠을 자는 바람에 기차를 놓쳤어요.** (I missed the train because I overslept.)
- **휴대폰이 고장 나는 바람에 연락을 못 했어요.** (I couldn’t contact you because my phone broke.)
- **앞차가 갑자기 멈추는 바람에 사고가 날 뻔했어요.** (Because the car in front stopped suddenly, I almost got into an accident.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip (Native Skill)**
> 
> 
> You CANNOT use this pattern for good results! For example, “친구가 도와주는 바람에 시험에 합격했어요” (I passed the exam because my friend helped me) sounds very awkward. In this case, you must use the positive cause pattern **“(N) 덕분에”** or **“(V)은/ㄴ 덕분에”**!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Make an excuse using an unexpected cause.

1. (I left my wallet at home so I couldn’t pay for the meal) 지갑을 집에 _______________ 밥값을 못 냈어요. (두고 오다)
2. (The alarm didn’t ring so I was late) 알람이 안 _______________ 지각했어요. (울리다 - Watch out for ‘ㄹ’ drop!)
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 두고 오는 바람에 / 2. 우는 바람에

### 9) My Sentence

Has a plan recently gone wrong because of a sudden event?

- 갑자기 _______________는 바람에 _______________았/었어요.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_87.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 형용사/동사 어간 Adjective/Verb Stem ]** +**을/ㄹ 텐데도**
- *“Even though it must be…”, “Despite the fact that it is probably…”*

### 2) Core Meaning

Used when you have fully guessed and expected the other person’s situation, but they act opposite to that obvious expectation. It’s a mature expression very often used to express apology or deep gratitude for the other person’s trouble or kindness.

### 3) Grammar

This is the “-을/ㄹ 텐데 (Strong guess)” from Pattern 55 combined with “도 (Concession)”!

- **If there is a Batchim:** -을 텐데도
- **If no Batchim or ‘ㄹ’ Batchim:** -ㄹ 텐데도
- For past events, use “-았/었을 텐데도”.

### 4) How it’s used

- When expressing gratitude: “피곤할 텐데도 와 주셔서 감사합니다.”
- When expressing surprise: “맛이 없을 텐데도 잘 먹네요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**피곤할 텐데도** (Even though you must be tired),**바쁘실 텐데도** (Even though you must be busy),**아플 텐데도** (Even though it must hurt)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **피곤할 텐데도** (Even though tired)
- **바쁘실 텐데도** (Even though busy)
- **아플 텐데도** (Even though hurting)
- **힘들 텐데도** (Even though difficult)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **피곤할 텐데도 와 주셔서 정말 감사합니다.** (Thank you for coming even though you must be tired.)
- **많이 바쁘실 텐데도 도와주셔서 고마워요.** (Thank you for helping me even though you must be very busy.)
- **제가 한 요리가 맛이 없을 텐데도 아주 잘 먹네요.** (Even though the food I cooked probably doesn’t taste good, they are eating it very well.)
- **그 일은 아주 힘들 텐데도 포기하지 않고 계속하네요.** (Even though that work must be very hard, he keeps doing it without giving up.)

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Express gratitude to someone who overturned your expectations.

1. (You received help knowing the other person was very busy) _______________ 저를 도와주셔서 감사합니다. (바쁘다)
2. (It was raining heavily but the other person came to the meeting place) 비가 많이 _______________ 와 주셔서 고마워요. (오다)
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 바쁘실 텐데도 / 2. 올 텐데도

### 9) My Sentence

Has a friend or colleague ever shown you kindness even in a difficult situation?

- _______________을/ㄹ 텐데도 저를 위해 _______________아/어 줘서 고마워요.

---

## 💎 Special Unit 8. 썸과 연애 (Korean Dating Culture)

"우리 사귈래?" (Shall we date?)

Korean dating culture is incredibly fast-paced, highly focused on communication frequency, and has unique milestones. If you are interested in dating a Korean, you must know these rules!

**1. 썸과 밀당 (The "Some" Phase & Mil-dang)**

*   **썸 (Some):** It comes from the English word 'Something'. It refers to the ambiguous stage where two people have romantic feelings, text frequently, and go on dates, but are not officially boyfriend/girlfriend yet. They are "riding a Some" (썸 타다).
*   **밀당 (Mil-dang):** Short for "밀고 당기기" (Push and pull). It's the strategic game of playing hard to get to increase the other person's interest.

**2. 고백 (The Confession: Day 1)**

In many Western cultures, casually hanging out eventually turns into a relationship. However, in Korea, a relationship officially begins ONLY after a clear**고백 (Confession)**! 

Someone must explicitly ask, "우리 사귈래?" (Shall we date?) or "내 여자친구/남자친구가 되어줄래?" (Will you be my girlfriend/boyfriend?). The day of the confession officially becomes "Day 1 (1일)" of the relationship.

**3. 연락 빈도와 "뭐해?" (Contact Frequency & "What are you doing?")**

In Korea, responding quickly to messages is considered a direct measure of affection. Texting each other from the moment you wake up until you fall asleep is very common. Also, when a Korean texts you "뭐해?" (What are you doing?), it often secretly means "I am thinking of you"!

**4. 100일과 커플템 (100 Days & Couple Items)**

Koreans celebrate relationships in 100-day increments (100일, 200일...). The 100th day is a huge milestone! Also, showing off your relationship by wearing matching clothes or shoes (커플룩 - Couple look) is a beloved culture.

**Practice**

✔**Practice 1.** Fill in the blanks with the correct dating terms.

1. (We are not dating yet, but we text every day and have feelings for each other) 우리 지금 _______________ 타는 중이에요.
2. (To officially start dating in Korea, you must do this) 오늘 밤에 그녀에게 _______________할 거예요!
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 썸 / 2. 고백

---


---


---



![[sulsul_pattern_88.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 형용사/동사 어간 Adjective/Verb Stem ]** +**기는(요)**
- *“Not at all…”, “It is hardly…”*

### 2) Core Meaning

This is the perfect expression to use when someone showers you with compliments and you want to reply shyly and humbly, saying “Eyy~ no I’m not~”. Or, it can be used to lightly and affectionately rebut someone’s statement, saying “That’s not true at all!”.

### 3) Grammar

Attach “-기는요” right after the Verb or Adjective Stem. It’s very easy because it doesn’t matter if there’s a Batchim or not!
When speaking, people often shorten it and say **“-긴요”**.

### 4) How it’s used

- When responding to a compliment: “한국말 잘하시네요!” → “잘하기는요. 아직 멀었어요.”
- When rebutting: “오늘 춥죠?” → “춥기는요. 하나도 안 추워요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**잘하기는요** (Good at it? Not at all),**예쁘기는요** (Pretty? Not at all),**바쁘긴요** (Busy? Not at all)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **잘하기는요.** (Not at all good at it.)
- **예쁘기는요.** (Not pretty at all.)
- **바쁘긴요.** (Not busy at all.)
- **춥긴요.** (Not cold at all.)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- A: **한국어를 정말 잘하시네요!**
B: **잘하기는요. 아직 멀었어요.** (Good at it? Not at all. I still have a long way to go.)
- A: **오늘 너무 춥죠?**
B: **춥기는요. 별로 안 추워요.** (Cold? Not really. It’s not that cold.)
- A: **저 때문에 많이 바쁘셨죠?**
B: **바쁘긴요. 언제든지 연락하세요.** (I’m not busy at all. Contact me anytime.)
- A: **그 옷 정말 비싸 보네요.**
B: **비싸긴요. 시장에서 엄청 싸게 샀어요.** (Expensive? No way. I bought it very cheaply at the market.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> When receiving a compliment, rather than just saying “감사합니다” (Thank you), playfully bouncing it back with “에이~ 잘하긴요” (Eyy~ I’m not that good) is the warm virtue of Korean humility.
> For reference, when attaching to a Noun, use **“(N)은/는요!”**. (e.g., 천재는요! = Genius? No way!)
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Humbly deny a compliment.

1. You cook really well!: 요리를 잘_______________. 그냥 인터넷 보고 했어요.
2. You must be tired, right?: _______________. 어제 잠을 많이 자서 괜찮아요. (피곤하다)
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 하기는요 (or 하긴요) / 2. 피곤하기는요 (or 피곤하긴요)

### 9) My Sentence

Imagine someone greatly complimented your strength, and reply politely and humbly.

- 에이, _______________기는요. 아직 부족해요.

## 

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_89.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 형용사/동사 어간 Adjective/Verb Stem ]** +**을/ㄹ 뿐만 아니라**
- *“Not only…, but also…”*

### 2) Core Meaning

Used when you want to incrementally state that ‘in addition to’ one feature or fact, there is ‘also’ another feature.
Most important point! When listing pros, a pro must follow; when listing cons, a con must follow for it to sound natural. (It feels like a perfect addition of A+B!)

### 3) Grammar

The rules are the same as making future tense modifiers.

- **If there is a Batchim:** -을 뿐만 아니라 (e.g., 좋다 →**좋을 뿐만 아니라**)
- **If no Batchim or ‘ㄹ’ Batchim:** -ㄹ 뿐만 아니라 (e.g., 예쁘다 →**예쁠 뿐만 아니라**)
- For past tense, use “-았/었을 뿐만 아니라”.
- After a Noun, just attach “뿐만 아니라”. (e.g., 학생뿐만 아니라 선생님도 왔어요.)

### 4) How it’s used

- When listing pros: “맛있을 뿐만 아니라 싸요.”
- When bad situations overlap: “비가 올 뿐만 아니라 바람도 불어요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**맛있을 뿐만 아니라** (Not only delicious, but also),**예쁠 뿐만 아니라** (Not only pretty, but also),**바쁠 뿐만 아니라** (Not only busy, but also)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **좋을 뿐만 아니라** (Not only good, but also)
- **맛있을 뿐만 아니라** (Not only delicious, but also)
- **예쁠 뿐만 아니라** (Not only pretty, but also)
- **비쌀 뿐만 아니라** (Not only expensive, but also)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **이 식당은 맛있을 뿐만 아니라 가격도 아주 싸요.** (This restaurant is not only delicious, but the price is also very cheap.)
- **지민 씨는 얼굴이 예쁠 뿐만 아니라 성격도 좋아요.** (Jimin is not only pretty, but she also has a good personality.)
- **오늘은 비가 올 뿐만 아니라 바람도 엄청 많이 불어요.** (Not only is it raining today, but it’s also very windy.)
- **그분은 노래를 잘 부를 뿐만 아니라 작곡도 직접 해요.** (Not only does he sing well, but he also composes his own songs.)

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Bundle two features at once and brag.

1. (Korean is fun. And it is practical) 한국어는 _______________ 실용적이에요. (재미있다)
2. (My puppy is cute. And it is smart) 우리 강아지는 _______________ 똑똑해요. (귀엽다 - Watch out for ㅂ irregular!)
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 재미있을 뿐만 아니라 / 2. 귀여울 뿐만 아니라

### 9) My Sentence

Brag about two charms of your favorite movie or singer in one sentence.

- _______________은/는 _______________을/ㄹ 뿐만 아니라 _______________.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_90.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **보고 싶어요** = *“I miss you”, “I want to see you”*
- **걱정돼요** = *“I’m worried”*
- **다행이에요** = *“That’s a relief”, “I’m glad”*

### 2) Core Meaning

These are the ‘Top 3 Emotional Expressions’ Koreans use the most every day! They are incredibly useful to pull out when you desperately miss someone, when your heart is anxious, or when you feel relieved because something you worried about turned out well.

### 3) Grammar

- **보고 싶어요:** When indicating the target, you can use the subject particle ‘-(이)가’ or the object particle ‘-을/를’. (e.g., 네가 보고 싶어 / 너를 보고 싶어 - Both are correct!)
- **걱정돼요:** You can attach it right after a Noun. (e.g., 시험이 걱정돼요)
- **다행이에요:** When stating the reason in the preceding sentence, the cause pattern ‘-아서/어서 다행이에요’ is frequently used.

### 4) How it’s used

- When you miss family or friends: “가족들이 보고 싶어요.”
- When you’re anxious things might go wrong: “내일 발표가 걱정돼요.”
- When someone is safe or an issue is resolved: “안 다쳐서 정말 다행이에요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**보고 싶어요** (I miss you),**걱정돼요** (I’m worried),**다행이에요** (That’s a relief)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **보고 싶어요.** (I miss you.)
- **진짜 보고 싶어요.** (I really miss you.)
- **걱정돼요.** (I’m worried.)
- **너무 걱정돼요.** (I’m so worried.)
- **다행이에요.** (That’s a relief.)
- **정말 다행이에요.** (I’m so relieved.)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **고향에 있는 가족들이 정말 보고 싶어요.** (I really miss my family back in my hometown.)
- **오랜만에 만난 친구가 너무 보고 싶었어요.** (I missed the friend I met after a long time so much.)
- **내일 중요한 발표가 있어서 너무 걱정돼요.** (I’m so worried because I have an important presentation tomorrow.)
- **사고가 났는데 아무도 안 다쳐서 정말 다행이에요.** (There was an accident, but I’m so glad/relieved nobody got hurt.)
- **비가 올까 봐 걱정했는데, 날씨가 맑아서 다행이에요.** (I was worried it might rain, but I’m relieved the weather is clear.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> Koreans say **“다행이다~”** almost like talking to themselves while sweeping their chests in relief. When something good happens to the other person or they avoid danger, try reacting with full soul by saying “와, 정말 다행이네요!”!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Express the emotion fitting the situation.

1. (You almost lost your wallet but found it in your bag): 와, 찾아서 정말 _______________!
2. (Tomorrow is your first Korean speaking test): 내일 시험이 너무 _______________.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 다행이에요 (or 다행이다) / 2. 걱정돼요

### 9) My Sentence

Has something you worried about recently resolved itself well, making you feel at ease?

- _______________아서/어서 정말 _______________.

---

 
![[sulsul_pattern_91.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **잘됐어요** = *“That’s great!”, “Good for you!”*
- **수고하셨어요** = *“Thank you for your hard work”, “Good job”*
- **힘내세요** = *“Cheer up!”, “Hang in there!”*

### 2) Core Meaning

These are the ‘Cheat Code Trio’ for social life and building strong human relationships! They are magical greetings used to congratulate someone like it’s your own business when you hear good news, acknowledge each other’s hard work when a task is done, and warmly support a friend having a hard time.

### 3) Grammar

There’s no need to analyze the grammar; just memorize and use them as fixed greetings as whole chunks.

- **잘됐어요:** A reaction when a task ends successfully or you hear good news!
- **수고하셨어요:** A must-have greeting when leaving work or when a meeting/class ends. For people higher in rank or age, using “고생하셨습니다” is more polite.
- **힘내세요:** Great to use as a pair with “파이팅(Fighting)!”.

### 4) How it’s used

- When a friend gets a good job: “와, 진짜 잘됐네요!”
- When finishing the day’s work: “오늘 하루도 수고하셨습니다.”
- When a friend is depressed after failing a test: “결과는 아쉽지만, 힘내세요!”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**잘됐어요** (That’s great),**잘됐네요** (Wow, that’s great),**수고하셨어요** (Good job),**고생하셨습니다** (Thank you for your hard work),**힘내세요** (Cheer up)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **잘됐어요!** (That’s great!)
- **정말 잘됐네요!** (That’s really great!)
- **수고하셨어요.** (Thank you for your hard work.)
- **고생하셨습니다.** (Thank you for your hard work - respectful.)
- **힘내세요!** (Cheer up!)
- **파이팅!** (You can do it!)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- A: **저 드디어 취업했어요!**
B: **와, 진짜 잘됐네요! 축하해요.** (Wow, that’s really great! Congratulations.)
- **오늘 하루도 수고하셨습니다. 내일 봬요.** (Thank you for your hard work today. See you tomorrow.)
- **결과가 안 좋아서 슬프지만, 힘내세요! 파이팅!** (I know you’re sad because the results weren’t good, but cheer up! Fighting!)
- **다음에 더 잘할 수 있을 거예요. 힘내요!** (You’ll do better next time. Hang in there!)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip (Preventing Fatal Mistakes!)**
> 
> 
> “수고하세요” (Present tense) is often used as a greeting to employees still working when you leave a store, right? However, **if you use this phrase to your boss or older adults at work, it can sound arrogant, so be absolutely careful!** To a boss, you must use "고생하셨습니다" or "노고가 많으셨습니다". Never use "수고하셨습니다" to your superiors!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Give a warm greeting fitting the situation.

1. (Towards the teacher right after the Korean class ends): 선생님, 오늘 수업 _______________!
2. (When a friend says they found their lost wallet): 와, 다 찾아서 정말 _______________!
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 수고하셨습니다 (or 고생하셨습니다) / 2. 잘됐어요 (or 잘됐네요)

### 9) My Sentence

If you have a friend who is exhausted and having a hard time right now, write down a word of encouragement.

- _______________아/어(Name), 너무 걱정하지 말고 _______________!

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_92.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **어쩔 수 없어요** = *“It can’t be helped”, “I have no choice”*
- **괜찮아질 거예요** = *“It will get better”, “Things will be okay”*

### 2) Core Meaning

There are so many things in life that don’t go our way, right? In those times, you use ‘어쩔 수 없어요’ to let go of lingering attachments and coolly move on saying, “Well, there’s no other way~”. On the contrary, when warmly comforting someone hurt by such events that things will get better as time passes, you use ‘괜찮아질 거예요’.

### 3) Grammar

- **어쩔 수 없어요:** It’s a shortened form of “어찌할 수 없다” (Cannot do anything about it). It’s an expression that has become very solidified in daily life.
- **괜찮아질 거예요:** It’s a very scientific and useful word of comfort that combines ‘괜찮다’ (Okay) + ‘-아/어지다’ (Change of state) + ‘-(으)ㄹ 거예요’ (Future).

### 4) How it’s used

- When resigning because you can’t change the situation: “비가 오니까 취소해요. 어쩔 수 없죠.”
- When easing the mind of a friend who made a mistake: “누구나 실수해요. 다 괜찮아질 거예요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**어쩔 수 없어요** (It can’t be helped),**어쩔 수 없지 뭐** (Well, what can you do),**괜찮아질 거예요** (It will get better)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **어쩔 수 없어요.** (It can’t be helped.)
- **어쩔 수 없죠.** (There’s nothing we can do about it.)
- **괜찮아질 거예요.** (It will get better.)
- **다 괜찮아질 거예요.** (Everything will be okay.)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **비가 오니까 오늘 야외 행사는 취소해야 해요. 어쩔 수 없어요.** (It’s raining, so we have to cancel the outdoor event today. It can’t be helped.)
- **다 처음이라서 그래요. 어쩔 수 없죠. 너무 자책하지 마세요.** (It’s because it’s your first time. There’s no helping it. Don’t blame yourself too much.)
- **시간이 지나면 다 괜찮아질 거예요. 울지 마세요.** (As time passes, everything will get better. Don’t cry.)
- **약 먹고 푹 자면 내일은 한결 괜찮아질 거예요.** (If you take medicine and get a good sleep, you will feel much better tomorrow.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> To a friend or someone very close, try coolly shrugging your shoulders and saying **“어쩔 수 없지 뭐~”** (Well, what can you do~) as if talking to yourself!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Complete the words of resignation and comfort.

1. (You went to a famous restaurant but it was closed because they ran out of ingredients): 아쉽지만 _______________. 다른 곳에 가요.
2. (When comforting a friend who fell and hurt their leg): 며칠 쉬면 다 _______________.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 어쩔 수 없어요 (or 어쩔 수 없죠) / 2. 괜찮아질 거예요

### 9) My Sentence

Write down a warm sentence you can say to yourself when you need comfort.

- 지금은 좀 힘들지만, 다 _______________.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_93.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **지금 가는 길이에요** = *“I’m on my way (there)”*
- **지금 오는 길이에요** = *“I’m on my way (here/back)”*
- **벌써 했어요** = *“I already did it”*

### 2) Core Meaning

This is a survival(?) pattern that answers perfectly when a friend you made an appointment with urges you asking “어디야?” (Where are you?)! Also, it’s very useful when someone asks you something and you proudly want to say, “Heh, I already finished it!”.

### 3) Grammar

- **가는 길이에요 / 오는 길이에요:** ‘-는 길(on the way)’ attaches to ‘가다/오다’ to vividly show that you are in the middle of moving towards a destination. You can also attach it directly after a Noun to say ‘Noun + 가는 길’ (e.g., 집 가는 길, 회사 가는 길).
- **벌써:** This is an adverb used when something finishes much earlier than expected. Since the action is already done, it MUST always be used with the past tense (했어요)!

### 4) How it’s used

- When heading to an appointment place: “미안해요, 지금 뛰어가는 길이에요!”
- When describing your commute home: “집에 오는 길에 빵을 샀어요.”
- When bragging about a task done in advance: “숙제 벌써 다 했어요!”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**가는 길이에요** (On my way there),**오는 길이에요** (On my way here),**벌써 도착했어요** (Already arrived),**벌써 끝났어요** (Already finished)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **가는 길이에요.** (I’m on my way there.)
- **오는 길이에요.** (I’m on my way here/back.)
- **퇴근하는 길이에요.** (I’m on my way home from work.)
- **벌써 다 했어요.** (I already did it all.)
- **벌써 끝났어요.** (It’s already finished.)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **조금 늦을 것 같아요. 미안해요, 지금 뛰어가는 길이에요!** (I think I’ll be a little late. I’m sorry, I’m running on my way there now!)
- **퇴근하고 집에 오는 길에 마트에서 빵을 샀어요.** (I bought bread at the mart on my way back home from work.)
- **지금 친구 만나러 가는 길인데 이따가 전화할게요.** (I’m on my way to meet a friend now, so I’ll call you later.)
- A: **숙제 다 했어요?**
B: **네, 벌써 다 했어요!** (Did you finish your homework? / Yes, I already finished it!)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> “가는 길이에요” is used as much as breathing while talking on the phone. If you are taking the bus, try combining the verbs to say **“지금 버스 타고 가는 길이에요”**! You will sound just like a native.
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** State your route and completion status.

1. (When a friend calls to ask where you are): 나 지금 거의 다 _______________! (가다)
2. (When told to come quickly so you’re not late for the movie): 걱정 마, 나 _______________ 영화관에 도착했어. (이미/생각보다 빨리)
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 가는 길이야 / 2. 벌써

### 9) My Sentence

What do you usually do on your ‘way back’ home from school or work?

- 저는 집에 _______________에 _______________.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_94.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **약간** = *“Slightly”, “A little bit”, “Kind of”*
- **좀** = *“A little”, “Please”, “Kinda”*

### 2) Core Meaning

To avoid speaking too directly to the other person, Korean native speakers sprinkle ‘약간’ and ‘좀’ at the front of their sentences a LOT like magical seasoning! The meaning of criticism or negative emotions softens and becomes a much rounder, truly Korean tone.

### 3) Grammar

- **약간:** Use it freely in front of a Noun or Adjective when expressing your feeling or opinion cautiously. (e.g., 약간 매워요).
- **좀:** This is a shortened form of ‘조금’. It can mean a small amount, but it is a powerful cushion word that softens the tone when declining or explaining a difficult situation.

### 4) How it’s used

- When expressing regret: “이거 약간 매운 것 같아요.” (They beat around the bush so the person who cooked won’t be hurt)
- When making an excuse: “오늘 좀 피곤해서 일찍 쉴게요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**약간 이상해요** (Kind of weird),**약간 커요** (Slightly big),**좀 비싸요** (A bit expensive),**좀 바빠요** (A bit busy)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **약간 이상해요.** (It’s kind of weird.)
- **약간 매워요.** (It’s slightly spicy.)
- **좀 비싸요.** (It’s a bit expensive.)
- **좀 바빠요.** (I’m a bit busy.)
- **좀 피곤해요.** (I’m a bit tired.)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **이 떡볶이는 제 입맛에는 약간 매운 것 같아요.** (I think this tteokbokki is a little bit spicy for my taste.)
- **그 사람은 스타일이 약간 연예인 같아요.** (That person’s style kind of looks like a celebrity.)
- **제가 오늘 몸이 좀 안 좋아서 약속을 미뤄야 할 것 같아요.** (I’m not feeling a bit well today, so I think I need to postpone our plans.)
- **이 옷은 저한테 좀 큰 것 같은데, 작은 사이즈 있어요?** (I think these clothes are a bit big for me, do you have a smaller size?)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> Unconditionally put **‘좀’** in when asking someone for a favor too! “이것 **좀** 주세요” gives off a much softer, polite vibe that feels less like a command compared to just “이것 주세요”.
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Change direct words into soft cushion words.

1. 이 옷은 비싸요 → 이 옷은 _______________ 비싸요.
2. 그 영화는 지루했어요 → 그 영화는 _______________ 지루했어요.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 좀 (or 약간) / 2. 약간 (or 좀)

### 9) My Sentence

Is there anything you see that is ‘slightly’ weird or that you ‘kinda’ want to change?

- 제 생각에 이것은 _______________ _______________.

## 💎 Special Unit 9. 번역 불가능한 한국의 감정 (Untranslatable Korean Emotions)

정 (Jeong), 눈치 (Nunchi), and 답답함 (Dapdaphada)

Language is a mirror reflecting culture. I will introduce some special emotional words unique to Koreans that are hard to translate perfectly into English. If you understand these words, you will be able to know the hearts of Koreans much more deeply!

**1. 정 (Jeong): The Invisible String**

It means the warm affection, bond, and sticky emotional connection between people that builds up as they spend a long time together. Even if you bicker and fight every day, over time an inseparable ‘Jeong’ forms, which is called ‘미운 정 (Miun Jeong / Hate-Jeong)’.

**2. 한 (Han): Deep Sorrow and Resilience**

It’s a complex, knotted emotion rooted in Korea’s history, mixing unfairness, sadness, and a strong will to overcome it. It is often expressed in traditional music like Pansori or in sad literature.

**3. 눈치 (Nunchi): The Sense to Read the Room**

It’s a high-level ability to quickly and accurately grasp people’s moods or the atmosphere of a situation without anyone saying a word! (It’s an essential weapon for social life!)

- **눈치가 빠르다:** A tactful person who is very good at reading the room.
- **눈치가 없다:** A person who is clueless and makes people around them uncomfortable by doing inappropriate things without ‘Nunchi’.

**4. 억울하다 (Eogulhada): When Your Heart Collapses**

It’s a very heartbreaking and maddeningly frustrating emotion you feel when you are misunderstood or subjected to unreasonable treatment despite doing absolutely nothing wrong, and have no way to explain yourself.

**5. 답답하다 & 시원하다 (The Temperature of the Soul)**

- **답답하다 (Dapdaphada):** It has a physical meaning like suffocating, but it also means the psychological “clogged” feeling you get when a situation is blocked and doesn’t go your way, or when someone can’t understand what you’re saying.
- **시원하다 (Siwonhada):** It means “Cool” like ice water, but Korean grandfathers will drink boiling hot soup and exclaim “크아~ 시원하다!” (Ah~ Siwonhada!). This doesn’t mean the soup’s temperature is cool; it’s an exclamation of the soul meaning that the blocked, stuffy feeling inside their heart is released and relieved along with their sweat!

**6. 애교 (Aegyo): Cuteness as a Weapon**

It’s a technique of radiating charm or getting someone to do a difficult favor by using an exaggerated, cutesy voice, facial expressions, and heart gestures. K-pop idols do it a lot, right? If used well, it disarms the heart, but if used poorly, it can make the other person feel ‘답답하게 (Dapdaphada)’!

**Practice**

✔**Practice 1.** Find the correct special Korean emotion.

1. (I immediately noticed that my boss was in a bad mood during the meeting just by looking at their face. I am a person with fast…) 저는 _______________가 빠른 사람이에요.
2. (I didn’t do anything wrong, but the teacher only scolded me. It’s really…) 정말 _______________.
3. (After sweating a lot in a hot sauna, my body feels very…) 뜨거운 사우나에 들어가서 땀을 쭉 빼고 나니까 몸이 아주 _______________.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 눈치 / 2. 억울해요 / 3. 시원해요
