![[sulsul_pattern_95.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **진짜** = *“Really”, “Truly”*
- **완전** = *“Totally”, “Completely” (Casual)*
- **너무** = *“Too”, “Very”, “So much”*

### 2) Core Meaning

When you absolutely cannot hide your emotions! These are the Top 3 emphasizing adverbs that amplify your feelings 200% when you eat something delicious or see something amazing. These are the words you hear the most in K-dramas, variety shows, and daily conversation!

### 3) Grammar

Just stick them right in front of the Adjective or Verb you want to emphasize.

- **너무:** Originally in Korean grammar, it was only used for negative situations (“너무 비싸요”), but people used it so much that the standard language rules changed to allow it freely for very good things too (“너무 예뻐요”)!
- **완전:** A very casual and hip expression used by young people or close friends when marveling at something.

### 4) How it’s used

- When marveling: “오늘 날씨 진짜 좋아요!”
- When strongly recommending: “이 영화 완전 재미있어요.”
- When you’re struggling and want to whine: “배가 너무 고파요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**진짜 좋아요** (Really good),**완전 대박** (Totally awesome),**너무 예뻐요** (So pretty),**진짜 맛있어요** (Truly delicious)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **진짜 좋아요!** (Really good!)
- **진짜 맛있어요!** (Truly delicious!)
- **완전 대박!** (Totally awesome!)
- **완전 예뻐요!** (Completely pretty!)
- **너무 비싸요!** (Too expensive!)
- **너무 피곤해요!** (So tired!)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **오늘 날씨 진짜 좋아요! 한강에 놀러 가고 싶어요.** (The weather today is really great! I want to go hang out at the Han River.)
- **이 식당 고기 진짜 맛있네요.** (The meat at this restaurant is truly delicious.)
- **이 영화 완전 재미있어요. 안 봤으면 꼭 보세요.** (This movie is totally fun. If you haven’t seen it, you must watch it.)
- **배가 너무 고파서 쓰러질 것 같아요.** (I’m so hungry I think I’m going to pass out.)
- **시험이 너무 어려워서 하나도 못 풀었어요.** (The exam was so difficult that I couldn’t solve a single question.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> When speaking with true emotion, drawing out the first syllable makes the feeling come alive. Like this: **“진~~~짜 맛있어요!”, ”완~~~전 대박!”**!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Explode your emotions and speak.

1. (You feel the best because you learned all 100 Korean patterns): 기분이 _______________ 좋아요!
2. (You slept late yesterday so you can’t open your eyes today): 오늘 _______________ 피곤해요.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 완전 (or 진짜/너무) / 2. 너무 (or 진짜/완전)

### 9) My Sentence

What is something recently that was so good or so hard that you thought “진짜, 완전, 너무”?

- 요즘 날씨가 _______________ _______________.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_96.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **원래** = *“Originally”, “By nature”, “Usually”*
- **사실** = *“Actually”, “In fact”*
- **솔직히** = *“Honestly”, “To be honest”*

### 2) Core Meaning

These are magical adverbs attached to the front of a sentence when carefully pulling out your true feelings or exciting behind-the-scenes stories that you had kept hidden. If you start a sentence with these words during a conversation, the listener’s eyes will sparkle and they will lean in thinking, “Oh? What is it?”!

### 3) Grammar

You can use them freely at the very front of the sentence or right after your name (the subject).

- **원래:** Used to emphasize the original nature/character of something, or that it has always been that way since before.
- **사실 / 솔직히:** Used to confess your true inner thoughts or hidden truths that others didn’t know.

### 4) How it’s used

- When stating your original taste: “저는 원래 매운 걸 잘 못 먹어요.”
- When delivering surprise news: “사실 저 남자친구 생겼어요!”
- When giving an honest evaluation: “솔직히 그 영화 별로였어요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**원래 그래요** (It’s originally like that),**사실은요** (Actually…),**솔직히 말하면** (To speak honestly)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **원래 그래요.** (It’s usually like that / It’s natural.)
- **원래 안 매워요.** (It’s originally not spicy.)
- **사실 저예요.** (Actually, it was me.)
- **사실 몰랐어요.** (In fact, I didn’t know.)
- **솔직히 별로예요.** (Honestly, it’s not very good.)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **저는 원래 매운 걸 잘 못 먹는데, 이건 맛있네요.** (I originally (naturally) can’t eat spicy food well, but this is delicious.)
- **사실 저 다음 달에 결혼해요!** (Actually, I’m getting married next month!)
- **사실 제가 그 케이크 다 먹었어요. 미안해요.** (Actually, I ate all of that cake. I’m sorry.)
- **솔직히 오늘 너무 피곤해서 집에 가고 싶어요.** (Honestly, I’m so tired today I want to go home.)
- **솔직히 말하면 그 사람 성격이 좀 이상한 것 같아요.** (To speak honestly, I think that person’s personality is a bit weird.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> When telling a huge secret, lower your voice slightly, say **“저기… 사실은요…” (sa-si-reun-yo…)**, and pause for about 3 seconds. It’s a 100% perfect native speaking technique!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Confess your hidden sincere feelings or original disposition.

1. (Everyone else says it’s delicious, but to my taste, it wasn’t good): _______________ 그 식당 별로였어요.
2. (I have a disposition where I don’t drink coffee since I was born): 저는 _______________ 커피를 안 마셔요.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 솔직히 (or 사실) / 2. 원래

### 9) My Sentence

Confess your “원래 (original)” taste or “솔직한 (honest)” feeling that others don’t know well.

- 저는 남들에게 말하지 않았지만, 사실 _______________.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_97.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **근데 있잖아요…** = *“By the way, you know what…”*
- **그러니까요** = *“Exactly!”, “That’s what I’m saying!”*

### 2) Core Meaning

These are the best ping-pong conjunctions for leading the mood of a conversation.
‘근데 있잖아요’ draws attention when abruptly changing the topic or starting a very interesting story, and ‘그러니까요’ is used to chime in and 200% empathize with the other person’s complaints or opinions.

### 3) Grammar

- **근데:** It is a shortened form of ‘그런데 (But/By the way)’.
- **있잖아요:** It originally comes from ‘있다 (Exist)’, but in conversation, it has become a complete idiomatic expression used simply to draw the other person’s attention like “Listen to me!”.
- **그러니까요:** It is a perfect one-sentence reaction used as an answer all on its own without needing any other words.

### 4) How it’s used

- When bringing up a new topic: “근데 있잖아요, 어제 무슨 일이 있었냐면요…”
- When strongly agreeing with someone: “내 말이 그 말이야! 그러니까요!”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**근데 있잖아요** (By the way, you know what),**그러니까요** (Exactly!)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **근데 있잖아요…** (By the way, you know what…)
- **근데 있잖아…** (반말 - By the way…)
- **그러니까요!** (Exactly!)
- **그러니까!** (반말 - I know right!)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **근데 있잖아요, 어제 민수 씨한테 무슨 일이 있었는지 알아요?** (By the way, do you know what happened to Minsu yesterday?)
- **그 이야기는 들었어요. 근데 있잖아요, 진짜 범인은 다른 사람이래요.** (I heard that story. But you know what, they say the real culprit is someone else.)
- A: **오늘 날씨 너무 덥고 습하지 않아요?**
B: **그러니까요! 가만히 있어도 땀이 나요.** (Exactly! I’m sweating even when doing nothing.)
- A: **그 식당은 다 좋은데 너무 비싸요.**
B: **그러니까요. 가격만 좀 내리면 완벽할 텐데.** (That’s what I’m saying. It would be perfect if they just lowered the price.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> Among close friends, drop the honorifics and say **“근데 있잖아…”** and **“그러니까!”**. These are the lines female leads use the most in cafe scenes of K-dramas or variety shows!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Complete the ping-pong conversation.

1. (When you suddenly remember a funny story during a conversation): 아! _______________, 어제 제가 길에서 연예인을 봤어요!
2. (When furiously empathizing with a friend complaining, “Everything goes up except my salary”): _______________! 살기가 너무 힘들어요.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 근데 있잖아요 / 2. 그러니까요

### 9) My Sentence

Do you have any interesting news that would grab your friend’s attention?

- 근데 있잖아요, _______________.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_98.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **딱 [ 명사 Noun ] 같아요.**
- *“It’s exactly like [Noun].”*

### 2) Core Meaning

Used when perfectly comparing an object to something else. ‘딱’ is a short, upbeat adverb meaning ‘exactly’ or ‘perfectly’, and putting this in a sentence makes the metaphor 10 times more vivid and fun!

### 3) Grammar

Just put the Noun you want to compare it to after ‘딱’ and attach ‘-같아요’, and you’re done! The form doesn’t change at all whether the Noun has a Batchim or not.

### 4) How it’s used

- When something hits your exact taste: “이거 딱 내 스타일 같아요.”
- When two things are so similar: “두 사람은 딱 형제 같아요.”
- When feeling the season or weather: “바람이 부는 게 딱 가을 같아요.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**딱 내 스타일** (Exactly my style),**딱 형제** (Exactly like brothers),**딱 가을** (Exactly like autumn)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **딱 내 스타일 같아요.** (It’s exactly my style.)
- **딱 가을 같아요.** (It feels exactly like autumn.)
- **딱 영화 같아요.** (It’s exactly like a movie.)
- **딱 그림 같아요.** (It’s exactly like a picture.)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **이 케이크 많이 달지 않고, 딱 내 스타일 같아요.** (This cake isn’t too sweet, it’s exactly my style.)
- **성격도 비슷하고 얼굴도 닮아서, 두 사람은 딱 형제 같아요.** (With similar personalities and looking alike, the two of them look exactly like brothers.)
- **하늘이 높고 시원한 게 날씨가 딱 가을 같아요.** (With the high sky and cool breeze, the weather feels exactly like autumn.)
- **창밖을 보니까 눈이 오는 게 딱 영화의 한 장면 같아요.** (Looking out the window, the falling snow looks exactly like a scene from a movie.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> When you try on clothes and the size fits perfectly as if made for you, you also use **“어머, 사이즈가 딱 맞아요!”** very frequently. Make sure to remember it!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Create a fitting metaphor.

1. (When you discover a bag with your absolute favorite design while shopping): 우와, 이거 _______________ 같아요.
2. (When looking at a pretty scenery): 풍경이 정말 아름다워요. _______________ 그림 같아요.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 딱 내 스타일 / 2. 딱

### 9) My Sentence

Is there a place that looks truly like a movie or a painting to you?

- 여기는 정말 아름다워서 _______________ 같아요.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_99.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 명사 Noun ]에 비하면 새 발의 피예요.**
- *“Compared to [Noun], it’s a drop in the bucket.” (Literal: blood of a bird’s foot)*

### 2) Core Meaning

“새 발의 피” means the blood coming from the foot of a very, very small sparrow—meaning the amount is ‘so small it’s nothing’. It’s a very fun and native-like advanced idiom used when you want to say that the hardship you just went through or the money you have is “nothing, it doesn’t even compare” to something massively huge!

### 3) Grammar

Write “-에 비하면” after the Noun that is the object of comparison (the massive standard), and attach “새 발의 피예요” as a whole phrase at the end of the sentence. Use ‘-에 비하면’ regardless of the Batchim.

### 4) How it’s used

- When comforting or encouraging: “오늘 고생은 어제에 비하면 새 발의 피예요.”
- When lowering yourself immensely and being humble: “제 실력은 선생님에 비하면 새 발의 피죠.”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**어제에 비하면** (Compared to yesterday),**서울 집값에 비하면** (Compared to Seoul’s housing prices),**너에 비하면** (Compared to you)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **어제에 비하면 새 발의 피예요.** (Compared to yesterday, it’s a drop in the bucket.)
- **너에 비하면 새 발의 피지.** (Casual - Compared to you, it’s nothing.)
- **한국어에 비하면 새 발의 피예요.** (Compared to Korean, it’s nothing.)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **오늘 과제는 어제 숙제 양에 비하면 진짜 새 발의 피예요. 금방 할 수 있어요.** (Today’s assignment is really a drop in the bucket compared to the amount of yesterday’s homework. I can do it quickly.)
- **제 요리 실력은 백종원 선생님에 비하면 새 발의 피예요.** (My cooking skills are nothing compared to Chef Baek Jong-won.)
- **서울 집값에 비하면 우리 동네 집값은 새 발의 피예요.** (Compared to Seoul’s housing prices, housing prices in our neighborhood are a drop in the bucket.)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> If you use this expression with perfect timing, your Korean friends will be 100% shocked and say, “Wow, where did you learn Korean? You’re practically Korean!”. Use it confidently and proudly!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Make a fuss by comparing it to a massive standard.

1. (Today’s heat is nothing compared to the heatwave of last August): 오늘 더위는 작년 여름_______________ 새 발의 피예요.
2. (Compared to your money, my money is like dust): 당신 재산_______________ 제 돈은 새 발의 피예요.
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 에 비하면 / 2. 에 비하면

### 9) My Sentence

Are there times when your worries or hardships feel tiny compared to something massive?

- 제 고민은 _______________에 비하면 새 발의 피예요.

---

 

![[sulsul_pattern_100.png|500]]
### 1) Pattern Definition

- **[ 동사 어간 Verb Stem ]** +**고 말았어요**
- *“Ended up doing…”, “Finally did…”*

### 2) Core Meaning

It’s a cinematic expression used to show **‘deep regret’** when you tried so hard to hold back but couldn’t and ended up doing it anyway, or the dramatic ‘sense of achievement’ bursting out when you finally accomplished something after a long and hard effort. The grand 100th pattern, it’s exactly the same emotion you are feeling right now!

### 3) Grammar

It can ONLY attach to Verbs. Use “-고 말았어요” unconditionally regardless of the Batchim.
Since it usually talks about the ‘result’ of an event, it is often used in the past tense, and it creates an absolutely perfect pairing with time adverbs like “결국 (finally)”, “드디어 (at last)”, and “마침내 (in the end)”.

### 4) How it’s used

- When you held back but crumbled: “다이어트 중인데, 결국 치킨을 먹고 말았어요.”
- When you achieved it after hard effort: “우리는 마침내 정상에 도착하고 말았어요!”

### 5) High-Frequency Words

**결국 먹고 말았어요** (Ended up eating in the end),**드디어 끝내고 말았어요** (Finally ended up finishing),**마침내 해내고 말았어요** (Finally ended up succeeding)

### 6) Basic Examples (Survival Level)

- **먹고 말았어요.** (Ended up eating.)
- **자고 말았어요.** (Ended up sleeping.)
- **사고 말았어요.** (Ended up buying.)
- **끝내고 말았어요.** (Ended up finishing.)
- **해내고 말았어요.** (Ended up succeeding / doing it.)

### 7) Expansion Examples (Natural Level)

- **다이어트 중인데, 냄새를 참지 못하고 결국 치킨을 먹고 말았어요.** (I’m on a diet, but I couldn’t resist the smell and ended up eating fried chicken.)
- **비싼 가격 때문에 고민했지만 결국 그 가방을 사고 말았어요.** (I hesitated because of the expensive price, but I eventually ended up buying that bag.)
- **우리는 험난한 산을 올라 마침내 정상에 도착하고 말았어요!** (We climbed the rugged mountain and finally reached the summit!)
- **우와, 기어코 100개의 패턴을 모두 다 끝내고 말았어요!** (Wow, we finally ended up finishing all 100 patterns!)
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **Teacher’s Tip**
> 
> 
> Everyone, you finally ended up completing all 100 core patterns of 『SULSUL Korean』! I am so proud of you for making it here without giving up halfway. Now, there will be nothing you can’t say in Korean. You truly worked hard!
> 

### 8) Practice

✔**Practice 1.** Express your final sense of achievement!

1. (It was so hard I wanted to give up, but finally I…) 너무 어려워서 포기하고 싶었지만, 마침내 한국어 100 패턴을 다 _______________! (끝내다)
2. (I studied hard and eventually…) 열심히 공부해서 결국 토픽 시험에 _______________! (합격하다)
> ✏️ **Answer Key**
> 
> 1. 끝내고 말았어요 / 2. 합격하고 말았어요

### 9) My Sentence

To all of you who have made it to the 100th pattern, leave your current feelings using this final pattern!

- 드디어 저는 한국어 100 패턴을 모두 _______________고 말았어요!

## 🏁 EPILOGUE. To You, Stepping Out into the Infinite Universe of Korean

**— Time to stop studying and start speaking ‘SULSUL’ (fluently)**

Everyone, you’ve truly worked hard!
From the very first word for survival to the 100th pattern detailing your deepest emotions, I am incredibly proud of you for not giving up and completing this journey. Now, the perfect ‘framework of language’ that Koreans use every single day has been built inside your head.

However, if you close the book here and call it a day, you might forget more than half of it by tomorrow morning. Because language is not about ‘understanding’ it with your head, but ‘acquiring’ it through your mouth and ears and completely ‘installing’ it into your body.

That is why I have prepared a perfect training system for you. Now is the time to stop studying at your desk and make Korean come out**‘SULSUL’ (fluently)** in your everyday life!

### **🚀 APPENDIX A. 28-Day Conversation Automation Masterplan & SULSUL App**

**— Automating your speaking routine in 4 weeks**

The fastest and most scientific way to download the 100 patterns you learned from this book into your brain is through repetitive training using the**Forgetting Curve**. For your perfect review, I created the official learning app,**[SULSUL Korean]**!

If you have finished reading this book, download the app (or visit the website) right now, and follow the 28-Day Masterplan below.

**[ 28-Day SULSUL Action Plan ]**

- **Week 1 (Input-Based):** Log into the SULSUL app and listen to the native audio for Patterns 01~25. Familiarize your ears with the rhythm and intonation. It’s okay if you don’t know the exact meaning. This is the week for riding the waves of sound.
- **Week 2 (Pattern-Based):** Look at Patterns 26~50 with your eyes and simultaneously spit them out with your mouth. Turn on the speaking feature in the app, check how close your pronunciation is to the native speaker’s rhythm, and assemble the blocks.
- **Week 3 (Output-Based):** Train with Patterns 51~75. From this point on, cover the screen, listen only to the Korean audio, and repeatedly do ‘Shadowing’ training where you speak immediately after hearing it.
- **Week 4 (Real Speaking):** After completing Patterns 76~100, turn on the [Infinite Loop Mode] on the app and leave it on until Korean just pops out naturally whenever you are nudged in your everyday life!

### **💎 APPENDIX B. My Sentence & Premium AI Coaching**

**— Perfecting the Korean nuances that foreigners never know**

What is the secret of people who really speak Korean “like a Korean”? It’s because they didn’t just use textbook examples, but created and used their own**‘Self-Sentences’** that contain their actual lives.

When your pattern proficiency reaches the ‘MASTER’ level in the SULSUL app, the**[Create My Sentence]** window will open with celebratory confetti. Use the pattern you learned today to write your real diary, emotions, or plans for tomorrow in Korean.

**[ SULSUL Premium AI Coaching ]**
Korean is a ‘language of relationship and emotion’ where the meaning can flip 180 degrees depending on a single particle (은/는/이/가), a tone of voice, or the level of intimacy. Aren’t you curious if the sentence you made really has the natural nuance Koreans use?

The sentences you input into the app can receive 1:1 feedback through**‘SULSUL Premium AI Coaching’**.
It’s not simply correcting wrong grammar. We send you a warm and perfect native nuance report as if I, Teacher Yona, am right beside you, saying, “In this situation, this tone sounds more polite and affectionate,” or “Koreans speak with this kind of nuance.”

To give wings to your Korean, start the premium coaching on the SULSUL app right now!

### **📚 APPENDIX C. Teacher’s Manual for Tutors & Language Exchange Partners**

**— Yona’s Teaching Method to Teach Korean the Fastest**

Are you not a student studying this book alone, but a tutor or a language exchange partner teaching Korean to someone else? If you want to elevate your student’s skills in the shortest amount of time, the way you teach must change.

**1. Sandwich Method (샌드위치 피드백)**
If you recklessly cut them off and correct them when their speaking is wrong, the student will close their mouth. Please correct them while protecting their self-esteem in the order of [Praise] → [Soft Correction] → [Encouragement].

**2. The Magic of 7 Seconds (7초의 마법 - Wait Time)**
Even if you ask a question and the student can’t answer, never give them the answer right away. The best tutor is one who silently waits for 7 seconds while the Korean language circuit is fiercely turning in the student’s head.

**3. Emotion-Based Speaking Induction (감정 기반 스피킹 유도)**
Don’t make them solve simple grammar problems. You have to ask questions that touch the student’s ‘real emotions’ like, “What was the thing that made you the angriest this morning?” for living Korean to come out.
> ![[pink_mascot.png|60]] **For Professional Tutors**

A perfect system is ready to give wings to your classes.

**Everyone, the moment you turn the last page of this book is the beginning of your true Korean journey. You don’t have to be perfect. You just need to be consistent. Until the day Korean comes out ‘SULSUL’ from your mouth, I, Yona, will always be with you!**

**With love, Yona.**
